## 9.4.1 NR-NTN GNSS resilience

9 Release 20 NR › 9.4 GNSS resilient NR-NTN operation

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | For GNSS-resilient NR-NTN initial access based on the transmission of two PRACH sequences using the same existing PRACH format and different roots (Alt 1-4), RAN1 to down-select one of the following alternatives:<br>- The transmission of the two PRACH sequences is performed in separate ROs in the time domain (TDM method). The transmission of the two PRACH sequences overlapping in time and frequency within the same RO (CDM method) is deprioritized in R20 NR NTN.<br>- Note: The above is decided based on the evaluation assumptions (such as LOS channel and 3 km/h) and studies during the corresponding study phase.<br>- FFS: Other details | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx) |
| Agreement | For the selection of the two root sequences in a dual PRACH transmission, RAN1 to specify a root-pairing mechanism. RAN1 to down-select one of the following options for the mechanism:<br>- Option A: Fixed/derived rule: The relationship between u1 and u2 is defined by a single, mathematical rule. Candidate rules include:<br>  - Option A-1: Inverse-Root Offset (Unit-Shift):<br>  - Option A-2: Logical Index Offset: e.g., i2 = (i1 + k) mod  or<br>  - Option A-3: Root-Group Offset:<br>  - Option A-4: ku1 = (k+1)u2 mod LRA, where k is an integer<br>- Option B:<br>  - Table-Based selection: The gNB signals an index or set of parameters that points to pre-defined table(s) of optimized root pairs.<br>    - Option B-1<br>      - Two tables are specified (for long and short preamble formats, respectively) with root pairs  selected to provide minimum CM difference within each pair while satisfying the root selection criterion , where ,   is the ZC sequence length and  is the maximum CFO normalized with respect to the subcarrier spacing. The root pairs in the tables are further ordered by ascending pair CM (defined as the max CM of the two roots in a pair).<br>      - The network indicates a starting logical root pair index from which the UE can extract up to 64 consecutive logical root pair indices from the applicable table.<br>      - Root pair tables (for evaluation purposes) are given in appendix B of R1-2608710,  R1-2607250<br>- Option C: Configured by NW:<br>  - Option C-1:<br>    - Use starting logical index i1 for the first root sequence and starting logical root index i2 for the second root sequence, using existing rules for preamble generation per RO<br>    - NW configure i1 and i2<br>  - Option C-2:<br>    - Use starting logical index i1 for the first root sequence and starting logical root index i2 for the second root sequence, root pair validation based on condition until determine 64 preamble pairs, where the condition is:<br>      - if the minimum Distance (Dmin) of a root pair is greater than d , the root pair is valid, otherwise the root pair is invalid.<br>      - the Dmin is calculated as  Dmin(s) = min-Kmax ≤ m ≤ Kmax  circ_dist(m·s, L), where<br>s = (u1-1 − u0-1) mod L, and circ_dist(x,L) = min(x mod L, L − (x mod L)), Kmax is 7, L is ZC sequence length, the value d is configured or pre-defined.<br>    - NW configure i1 and i2<br>- Option D:<br>  - NW configure root pair list, where each entry of the list is a pair of root (u1,u2).<br>Companies are encouraged provide evaluation if relevant for MDR, CM/PAPR, time/frequency estimation errors and signaling overhead, inter-cell PRACH interference, etc.<br>- The exact parameters, value ranges, and signaling for the chosen mechanism (e.g., values for s or k, or the design of the tables).<br>- The handling of the cyclic shift for the first and second PRACH transmission.<br>- The impact on required number of correlators per RO, considering the RO pairing mechanism that will be used. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607814](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607814.zip) |
| Agreement | RAN1 to down-select one of the following granularity frameworks for the FAC in Msg2:<br>- Option 1: SCS-based Granularity (WF1): The FAC granularity is defined relative to the subcarrier spacing.<br>    - FAC_granularity = SCS / 2k<br>    - k = [8]<br>  - Note : The FAC is applied as an offset to the nominal UL carrier frequency  derived from uplink config.<br>- Option 2: PPM-based Granularity (WF2): The FAC granularity is defined as an absolute value in ppm<br>    - X = [0.01] ppm<br>- Option 3 (compromised): SCS-based Granularity (WF1): The FAC granularity is defined relative to the subcarrier spacing.<br>    - FAC_granularity = SCS / 2k<br>    - k is selected such that the granularity when converted to ppm  is less than 0.01 ppm.<br>  - Note : The FAC is applied as an offset to the nominal UL carrier frequency  derived from uplink config. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607814](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607814.zip) |
| Agreement | For of dual PRACH transmission attempt:<br>- One RACH attempt consists of one " RO pair " or more than one RO pair (in case PRACH Repetition is used):<br>  - an "RO-Pair" is consisting of two valid ROs to accommodate the dual PRACH transmission.<br>    - FFS: Whether the “RO pair” will be defined in the specs<br>  - The RO-pair is located on the same frequency resources.<br>  - The two ROs in the pair occupy different time-domain resources.<br>  - FFS: How to perform Dual PRACH transmission with repetition<br>  - FFS: configuration details | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607814](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607814.zip) |
| Agreement | - RAN1 to discuss and to down-select one of the following alternatives for determining the temporal relationship between the two ROs of one RO-pair, when the PRACH repetition is not used:<br>  - Alternative 1: Consecutive ROs: The two ROs are two consecutive valid ROs in the time domain, based on the PRACH configuration index/a SSB.<br>    - “Valid ROs” per existing specs is the baseline.<br>    - FFS: Whether additional rule to validate RO for dual PRACH transmission should be defined<br>  - Alternative 2: Configurable offset: The second RO is located at a configured time offset relative to the first RO.<br>    - First RO is configurated per the current specs (based on PRACH config index), and<br>    - The second RO is the newly added RO which determined based on the configurable offset, and<br>    - The offset is set to be larger than zero, and<br>    - First RO of the RO-pair is a “Valid RO” per existing specs<br>    - FFS: Whether/how to determine the validity of second RO<br>- Whether to support/configure separate/shared RO resources for GNSS-resilient UEs is discussed in a separate proposal.<br>- SSB-to-RO mapping rules will be discussed in a separate proposal | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607814](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607814.zip) |
| Agreement | For handling of TA over-compensation (i.e., the UE's signal arrives earlier than expected) in initial access for GNSS resilience operation, support the following:<br>- Introduce a TAC field in the MAC RAR to directly indicate positive or negative timing corrections relative to the UE's open-loop pre-compensated transmission.<br>- Introduce a higher-layer configured TA offset. | Agreement | The Frequency Adjustment Command (FAC) in connected mode is indicated explicitly via a new MAC CE<br>- Details of the MAC CE structure to be coordinated with RAN2.<br>The closed-loop frequency control utilizes an accumulative (relative) adjustment mechanism as the baseline.<br>- Baseline principle: The UE updates its closed-loop frequency adjustment state by adding the relative delta value indicated in the FAC MAC CE to its current state.<br>FFS:<br>- The range for the relative FAC in the MAC CE<br>- UE behavior regarding the accumulated FAC/TAC state upon a change in the open-loop pre-compensation reference or satellite handover (e.g., retain, rebase, or reset). | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607814](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607814.zip) |
| Agreement | For TA control in RRC_CONNECTED for GNSS-resilient NR-NTN operation, RAN1 to discuss and down-select one of the following for the implicit TA drift compensation:<br>  - Option 1: Explicitly define the mathematical formulas (e.g. NTA(t)=NTA(t0) + TAdrift ×(t−t0)) and the exact reference time (t0​) in the 3GPP specifications (TS 38.211 / 38.213).<br>    - FFS: How to derive TA drift from the FAC.<br>  - Option 2 (UE Implementation): Leave the specific derivation of TA drift from the FAC and its continuous application up to UE implementation, provided the UE meets applicable RAN4 performance requirements.<br>FFS:<br>- State management upon command reception or reference change: The exact UE behavior (e.g., retain, rebase, or reset to zero) for the accumulated TA, FAC, and TA drift states upon reception of a new TAC/FAC or when the open-loop compensation basis changes (such as a change in the network-indicated reference location or during a satellite handover). | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607814](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607814.zip) |

Support separate PRACH configurations for Rel-20 GNSS-resilient initial access to ensure coexistence with legacy NTN UEs.<br>FFS: Whether/how to handle the case where RO for dual PRACH and RO for single PRACH transmission are overlapped in time and frequency. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607816](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607816.zip) |
| Agreement | Reuse the legacy SSB-to-RO mapping rules and configurations to map the selected SSB(s) to the designated RO-Pair. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607816](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607816.zip) |
| Agreement | For the dual PRACH transmission feature, where one RACH attempt consists of two PRACH transmissions in an RO pair:<br>- the two PRACH transmissions are treated as a single random-access attempt.<br>  - Note: This implies a single preamble transmission counter increment and a single power ramping counter increment for the attempt.<br>- the RAR window timing and the RA-RNTI calculation are based on the last of the two ROs in the pair.<br>- no power ramping is applied between the two PRACH transmissions within a single attempt. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607816](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607816.zip) |

## 10.2.1 Waveform

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.2 Waveform for 6GR air interface

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Conclusion | UL low PAPR enhancement for 16QAM is not pursued. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx) |
| Conclusion | For low UL PAPR enhancement:<br>- The same transmit filter is applied to both DMRS symbols and data symbols.<br>- It is RAN1 understanding that utilizing the knowledge of the transmit filter in the receiver processing is an implementation choice.<br>- It is up to RAN4 how to specify filter characterizations, e.g. by defining the spectrum mask.<br>  - Note: it is RAN1 understanding that the alignment on usage of specified filter characterizations between aNB and UE is possible. This could be achieved e.g. by corresponding UE-capability indication and network activating the feature through RRC configuration<br>- RAN1 will not define transmit filter coefficients in RAN1.<br>RAN1 will provide information of the steep roll-off filters that were used in the evaluations to RAN4 for information. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607942](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607942.zip) |

## 10.3.2 Modulation

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.3 Channel coding and modulation for 6GR interface

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | Use NR DL 1K QAM MCS entries (in TS 38.214 Table 5.1.3.1-4) as a reference for design of the UL 1K QAM MCS table, at least for CP-OFDM. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |
| Agreement | For performance study to allow a single spectrum efficiency point (subject to quantization error) to be supported by multiple  pairs, both fixed SE simulations and simulations with link adaptation are needed.<br>- The performance study is to identify if the preferred  pair changes with respect to channel profile, rank, spatial-layer imbalance, receiver type, Doppler, EVM, UL power back-off, and LDPC code<br>  - FFS: Other factors that may affect the preferred  pair choice<br>- For channels to be evaluated<br>  - The set of fading channels include: CDL-A 100ns, CDL-B 30ns, CDL-C 300ns, CDL-D 30ns<br>    - Other fading channel can be included in the set, per company report<br>  - The set of MIMO schemes include: SIMO 1x4 (UL), MIMO 4x4 (DL), MIMO 2x4 (UL), MIMO32x4 (DL), MIMO2x32 (UL)<br>    - Other MIMO schemes can be included in the set, per company report<br>- Precoder assumption: Closed loop precoder. Open loop precoder for high Doppler channels (at least 200Hz).<br>- CW to layer mapping: NR CW to layer mapping as baseline.<br>  - Other CW to layer mapping can be reported<br>- LDPC: NR BG1/BG2 as baseline<br>  - BG3 results can be reported.<br>- For UL, both DFTs and CP-OFDM waveform can be evaluated<br>  - Other waveform can be reported<br>- For fixed SE simulations<br>  - The SE points of current NR are used as starting point of the evaluation. For each SE point, can evaluate MCS with one step higher modulation order (if applicable) and MCS with one step lower modulation order (if applicable), with coding rate adjusted to maintain the SE to be the same (subject to quantization error).<br>  - If more than one step higher/lower modulation order evaluated, please add more columns to the table<br>- For link level simulation with link adaptation (prioritize fading channel) for a set of fading channels and MIMO schemes:<br>  - Performance report in the form of SNR and the corresponding achieved spectrum efficiency with and without a single spectrum efficiency point to be supported by multiple  pairs<br>  - Report the MCS table used with one single spectrum efficiency point supported by multiple  pairs<br>  - Report the SNR for rank switching points with rank adaptation<br>  - Report the supporting CQI design and/or mechanism for gNB to select between different MCS entries corresponding to the same SE point.<br>- When reporting results, please also provide<br>  - If applicable, EVM, power back-off assumption for each modulation order or each MCS<br>  - Receiver used (MMSE or rML(DL))<br>  - Demapping/decoding complexity difference with respect to NR MCS for fixed SE evaluations | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2608656](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2608656.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |

## 10.4.1 Operation in idle mode

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.4 Multiple carrier operation

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | Update the following agreement agreed in RAN1#126 by adding Note 4 in red. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in idle mode]` |
| Agreement | For study of multiple carriers where for initial access a UE can utilize resources for UL (Msg1, etc) or for both UL (Msg1, etc.) and DL (Msg2, etc.) in one carrier other than the carrier where the UE obtains the system information providing configuration for those resources, consider the following potential scenarios for the set of candidate physical carriers for which resources are provided for initial access:<br>  - S1: 1 DL physical carrier + N UL physical carriers, where N > 1<br>  - S2: M DL physical carriers + 1 UL physical carrier, where M > 1<br>  - S3: M DL physical carriers + N UL physical carriers, where M > 1 and N > 1<br>Note 1: The carrier where the UE obtains the system information and other candidate physical carrier(s) are counted above.<br>Note 2: For a given UE and a given initial access procedure, UE may use only a subset of the candidate physical carrier.<br>Note 3: The inclusion of a scenario for study does not imply agreement to support it. The need and applicable use cases/deployment scenarios for each scenario are subject to further study.<br>Note 4: For S2 and S3, M DL physical carriers may include carrier(s) only providing reference signals for initial-access UL transmissions on candidate UL carriers. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in idle mode]` |
| Conclusion | For UC-1 (UL coverage enhancement / DL–UL coverage imbalance), compared with single-carrier initial access on a mid-/high-frequency carrier, idle/inactive (if supported) mode multi-carrier operation which enables Msg1/Msg3 transmission on a lower-frequency UL carrier while retaining DL reception on the mid-/high-frequency carrier can provide benefit in terms of coverage of UL transmission of initial access for UEs with sufficient DL coverage but insufficient UL coverage on the mid-/high-frequency carrier. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in idle mode]` |
| Agreement | For further study of multiple carriers where for initial access a UE can utilize resources for UL (Msg1, etc) or for both UL (Msg1, etc.) and DL (Msg2, etc.) in one carrier other than the carrier where the UE obtains the system information providing configuration for those resources, study the following combinations of scenarios and deployment conditions, with the associated study aspects:<br>  - S1 with D1<br>    - Study the following aspects:<br>      - UL access carrier selection<br>      - Reference signal-to-RO mapping<br>      - Spatial relation determination for UL transmissions<br>      - UL Tx power control (e.g., pathloss calculation/estimation)<br>    - FFS whether or not S1 with D1 is applicable to S1 with D2/D3<br>  - S3<br>    - Consider the following sub-scenarios:<br>      - S3a: One UL carrier from N UL carriers is selected for UL (Msg1, etc.) initial access transmissions. Only the SI-providing DL carrier is used for initial-access DL transmissions (e.g., Msg2/Msg4), while DL reference signals on other DL carriers can be used by the UE, e.g. for sync and/or pathloss estimation purposes for initial-access UL transmissions<br>      - S3b: One UL carrier from N UL carriers is selected for UL (Msg1, etc.) initial access transmissions. The DL carrier associated with the selected UL carrier is used for initial-access DL transmissions (e.g., Msg2/Msg4)<br>    - Study the following aspects:<br>      - UL access carrier selection<br>      - Reference signal-to-RO mapping<br>      - Synchronization acquisition including RS assumptions on multiple DL carriers<br>      - UL transmission timing determination and TA acquisition<br>      - Spatial relation determination for UL transmissions<br>      - UL Tx power control (e.g., pathloss calculation/estimation)<br>      - UE measurement behaviours<br>      - NW/UE energy efficiency<br>      - Association between DL carrier(s) and UL carrier(s)<br>      - Detailed procedure compared to single-carrier based cell reselection<br>      - Applicability to D1/D2/D3<br>- Candidate carrier information and the associated initial access resource configuration is provided via SI on the SI-providing carrier.<br>Note 1: FFS whether S2 can be accommodated as a special case of S3 with a single candidate UL carrier, without requiring separate design.<br>Note 2: Other combinations of scenarios and deployment conditions are not precluded from further study, subject to justification of their applicability and additional benefits.<br>Note 3: The inclusion of a combination for study does not imply agreement to support it. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in idle mode]` |

## 10.4.2 Operation in connected mode

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.4 Multiple carrier operation

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | For study of flexible utilization of spectrum resources for DL and UL for connected mode over different carriers/bands, considering at least the following:<br>- Applicable scenario(s) and condition(s), e.g.,<br>  - Applicable collocation conditions, including collocated and/or non-collocated<br>  - Applicable timing/frequency synchronization relationship, e.g., D1/D2/D3 as agreed in RAN1#126 under 10.4.1<br>- Detailed study objectives in respective scenario(s) and condition(s), e.g.,<br>  - Synchronization acquisition<br>  - TA determination<br>  - Spatial filter determination<br>  - UL Tx power control (e.g., pathloss calculation/estimation)<br>  - Under which scenario(s)/condition(s) concurrent UL transmission and/or DL reception across the considered multiple carriers are allowed or not<br>  - Under which scenario(s)/condition(s) UL Tx switching across multiple carriers are allowed or not<br>  - Applicable DL/UL signals/channels<br>  - Scheduling carrier(s) for the UL/DL carrier(s)<br>  - Other objectives not precluded<br>- Detailed cases, e.g.,:<br>  - Case 1: 1 DL carrier and 1 UL carrier, where the DL and UL carrier are frequency separated, and no other DL carrier or UL carrier is configured for a UE<br>  - Case 2: 1 DL carrier and N UL carriers (N>1), where at least 1 UL carrier is frequency separated from the DL carrier, and no other DL carrier is configured for a UE<br>  - Case 3: M DL carriers (M>1) and 1 UL carrier, where at least 1 DL carrier is frequency separated from the UL carrier, and no other UL carrier is configured for a UE<br>  - Case 4: M DL carriers (M>1) and N UL carriers (N>1), where at least one of the following conditions is met:<br>    - At least 1 DL carrier is frequency separated from all the UL carriers<br>    - At least 1 UL carrier is frequency separated from all the DL carriers<br>  - Case 5: M DL carriers (M>1) and N UL carriers (N>1), where all the following conditions are met<br>    - For each DL carrier, at least one of UL carriers is not frequency separated from the DL carrier<br>    - For each UL carrier, at least one of DL carriers is not frequency separated from the UL carrier<br>    - At least one DL carrier is frequency separated from at least one UL carrier<br>  - Case 6: M DL carriers (M≥1) and N UL carriers (N≥1), where all the following conditions are met<br>    - For each DL carrier, all of UL carriers are not frequency separated from the DL carrier<br>    - For each UL carrier, all of DL carriers are not frequency separated from the UL carrier<br>  - Note: For above cases, study applicablity of Method 1, Method 2<br>  - FFS possible frequency separation in each of the cases from RAN1 point of view<br>  - Note: For the discussion purpose,<br>    - In FDD and TDD in 5G NR, DL carrier and UL carrier are regarded as not frequency separated<br>    - Consequently, in intra-band CA in 5G NR, DL/UL carriers are regarded as not frequency separated | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-MC-Operation in connected mode]` |

Option 1: 1% for PSS<br>  - Option 2: 1% for PSS&SSS<br>- Miss detection rate (MDR) for PSS&SSS: 1%<br>  - The detection is claimed to be successful only when both PSS and SSS are correctly detected and the detected timing is within half CP length compared to the strongest path of the channel.<br>  - [False timing performance for 1% FAR]<br>    - [Number of peaks exceeding the threshold]<br>  - Residual timing error: RMSE or 95th percentile<br>  - Residual frequency offset: RMSE or 95th percentile<br>- Detection complexity<br>  - PSS detection complexity: total # of real value multiplications for one detection attempt across M repetitions within N SSB periodicities<br>    - [Note: companies are encouraged to report the number of PSS peak positions in the evaluation, which are passed to SSS for further detection]<br>  - SSS detection complexity: total # of real value multiplications for one detection attempt for one detection attempt across M repetitions within N SSB periodicities<br>  - [Memory usage: the amount of memory for PSS and SSS detection]<br>Note: companies are encouraged to report the PSS/SSS detection procedure and algorithms, interference modeling for the above evaluation.<br>Note: to evaluate PSS/SSS combining across multiple SSB occasions,<br>- Option 1 and Option 3 are recommended (referring to the 6 SSB periodicity options) for the number of SSB repetitions and number of SSB periodicities for combining.<br>- Companies are encouraged to model the sampling frequency offset (SFO). | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |

## 10.5.1.2 PRACH and RACH procedure

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.5 Multi-antenna system › 10.5.1 Initial access and mobility

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | In addition to uniform SSB-to-RO mapping, study non-uniform SSB-to-RO mapping, and other reference signal-to-RO mapping (if necessary).<br>Chair note: Msg 3 payload size and associated coverage study evaluations are expected to be handled under 10.5.1.1 | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
| Agreement | Study requirements, need, justification, and new/enhanced formats to address scenarios not covered by the NR, including:<br>- Potential format requirements addressing extreme Doppler, high-speed, and GNSS-degraded/less NTN conditions, inter-cell interference.<br>  - Different CP length, sequence length, subcarrier-spacing, and/or sequence repetition of formats (relative to a NR preamble format) balancing preamble duration, coverage, Doppler robustness, resource overhead, detection complexity, and capacity (e.g., around 4-7 GHz);<br>  - Other potential change | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
| Agreement | - Adopt 4-step RACH as the baseline procedure for 6GR.<br>- Study 2-step RACH, RACH-less/contention-based-PUSCH access for UEs with adequate uplink timing alignment, dedicated RACH (1-step) as additional procedure(s).<br>- Study for 2-step RACH to include aspects asked by RAN2 in LS R1-2605183/R2-2604385<br>- Justifications of the potential additional procedure(s) | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
| Agreement | For RAN2 LS reply on random access in 6G [R1-2605183/R2-2604385]:<br>- RAN1 has not yet studied the design of 2-step RA for 6GR and the answers to Q1 and Q2 are based on 5G NR 2-step design which may or may not be applicable for 6GR. The following is agreements regarding RA procedure in RAN1:<br>\| Agreement Adopt 4-step RACH as the baseline procedure for 6GR. Study 2-step RACH, RACH-less/contention-based-PUSCH access for UEs with adequate uplink timing alignment, dedicated RACH (1-step) as additional procedure(s). Study for 2-step RACH to include aspects asked by RAN2 in LS R1-2605183/R2-2604385 Justifications of the potential additional procedure(s) \|<br>- Question 1 (whether it is possible to support 2-step and 4-step RA when they are both configured with the same preamble and RACH occasion):<br>  - RAN1 has not yet studied 2-step RA design and not yet assessed feasibility for 6GR. RAN1 has identified at least the following aspects based on 5G NR 2-step Preamble and MsgA:<br>    - The gNB attempts PUSCH reception for a detected preamble and falls back to 4-step RA when the PUSCH is not successfully received, which increases the number of blind PUSCH detections at the gNB.<br>    - If 4-step RA is configured together with 2-step RA, the response for a 4-step RA UE may need to be delayed until after the PUSCH occasion, which may increase 4-step RA latency and/or reduce the resources available for RAR transmission within the response window.<br>    - Distinguishing which procedure a UE has initiated is not deterministic from the preamble alone, which has implications on RAR/fallback handling.<br>- Question 2 (for 2-step RA, whether it is necessary to have correspondence between PRACH and PUSCH transmissions in MsgA, and if so how to establish it):<br>  - From RAN1 perspective, correspondence is necessary, at least for the following functions based on 5G NR 2-step Preamble and MsgA:<br>    - determination of the PUSCH resource associated with a detected preamble,<br>    - PUSCH scrambling and multiplexing of UEs sharing an RO,<br>    - timing-advance handling, and identification of the UE in the fallback RAR.<br>  - Note that RAN1 has not made any agreements on physical signals/channels of 2-step RA for 6GR and applicability of correspondence is currently undetermined.<br>- Note: RAN1 will continue the study of the above aspects and will inform RAN2 if the answers need to be updated. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
| Agreement | Draft LS R1-2608787 is endorsed in principle | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
| Agreement | Final LS R1-2608788 is endorsed. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |

| Conclusion | There is no consensus to support modulation orders other than QPSK for Rel-21 6GR PDCCH. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2608524](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2608524.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | For MRSS, support dynamic sharing of time-frequency resources of 5G NR and 6GR PDCCH transmission, from the NW perspective in a way transparent to the UE:<br>- When dynamic sharing of time-frequency resources of 5G NR and 6GR PDCCH transmission, NR PDCCH and 6GR PDCCH are transmitted in shared time/frequency resources.<br>  - Study the following:<br>    - FFS applicable scenarios for fully and/or partially overlapping CORESETs<br>    - FFS 6GR CCE-to-REG mapping, REG/CCE indexing, resource alignment, and hashing function for efficient PDCCH resource sharing between 5G NR and 6GR when 6GR and NR CORESETs fully or partially overlap. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2608525](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2608525.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | For the FFS on a need for REG-bundle sizes other than 6 REGs consider the following options:<br>- Option 1: 24 REGs<br>- Option 2: 12 REGs<br>- Option 3: 3 REGs<br>- Option 4: 2 REGs<br>- Option 5: other than 6 REGs is not needed.<br>- FFS conditions for applicability | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2608526](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2608526.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |

| Agreement | At least for single carrier operation, For the study of the maximum number of RBs in a 6GR PDCCH CORESET in frequency domain, consider the following options:<br>- Option 1: number of RBs does not need to be restricted explicitly. That is the number of RBs can be up to the maximum carrier bandwidth<br>- Option 2: The number of RBs is smaller than the maximum carrier bandwidth:<br>  - Option 2a: The number of RBs in CORESET can be up to 200 MHz<br>  - Option 2b: The number of RBs in CORESET can be up to 100 MHz<br>  - Option 2c: Other values for the maximum number of RBs in CORESET | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2608526](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2608526.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | For the further study of the CCE-to-REG mapping for overlapping CORESETs (e.g., wideband and narrowband 6GR CORESETs, and NR and 6GR CORESETs for MRSS), consider the following as starting point:<br>- Option 1: Only one CCE-to-REG mapping is applied for a 6GR CORESET, PDCCH candidates are mapped only to a single CORESET, independently if CORESETs partially overlap, in a way transparent for the UE.<br>- Further discuss necessity and feasibility of the following options including UE transparency aspects .<br>  - Option 2: Only one CCE-to-REG mapping is applied for a 6GR CORESET, but PDCCH candidate can use CCEs of more than one of the non-overlapping CORESETs aggregated in time and/or frequency<br>    - FFS whether the same CCE-to-REG mapping scheme should be used for all aggregated CORESETs or not.<br>    - FFS the maximum number of aggregated CORESETs.<br>    - FFS whether CORESETs should be adjacent to each other in time/frequency or not.<br>  - Option 3: Same CCE-to-REG mapping scheme (interleaved or non-interleaved) is independently applied to multiple sets of resources within 6GR CORESET<br>    - The sets do not overlap in both time and frequency<br>      - FFS how to define time-frequency resources per set<br>    - Option 3a: The sets are multiplexed in frequency<br>    - Option 3b: The sets are multiplexed in time<br>    - FFS whether a PDCCH candidate can use CCEs of more than one sets of resources.<br>    - FFS: whether the sets of resources are multiplexed in both time and frequency<br>    - FFS: the maximum number of sets in a 6G CORESET | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2608526](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2608526.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |

Receiver options (in R1-2509598) Option 1: AI-based denoise on DMRS REs only Option 2: AI-based channel estimation for data REs (with or without denoise on DMRS REs) Option 3: AI based channel prediction for target REs Option 4: AI-based joint {channel estimation + equalization + demodulation/LLR calculation} Option 5: AI-based {channel estimation} + AI-based {equalization + demodulation/LLR calculation} Additional Receiver options Option 6: Channel estimation + equalization + AI-based {demodulation/LLR calculation} Option 7: AI-based {Channel estimation} + equalization + AI-based {demodulation/LLR calculation} Other receiver options are not precluded Note: Receiver type (such as AI/non-AI) including the above Options in aNB will not be specified. \|<br>\| #2 Model input \| Reported by company, including input content and input dimension \|<br>\| #3 Model output \| Reported by company, including output content and output dimension \|<br>\| #4 Label \| If applicable, reported by company, including label acquisition method and label error (e.g., zero for ideal label, or non-zero for realistic label) (Labelling error model can be reported optionally) (Cost function can be reported optionally) \|<br>\| #5 Training type \| Offline \|<br>\| #6 Number of MIMO layers \| Reported by company \|<br>\| #7 Number of FLOPs (for receiver block replaced by AIML model) \| Reported by company Company also reports Inference related parameters for PUSCH (e.g., number of RBs, symbols) (“Number of FLOPs per RB/slot/layer” or “Number of FLOPs per RB/slot/layer/Rx Antenna” can be reported optionally) \|<br>\| #7-1 Number of FLOPs (for receiver block not replaced by AIML model) \| Number of FLOPs of non-AI receiver blocks corresponding to the candidate receiver assumption(s) in #1 can be reported by company (optional). (if reported, companies to report how to calculate Number of FLOPs of non-AI receiver blocks as well) Note: Non-AI receiver blocks can be: channel estimation, channel interpolation, channel equalization, and demodulation/LLR calculation. (FLOPs of additional pre/post processing can be reported optionally, if any) \|<br>\| #8 Number of parameters of AI Model \| Reported by company (Backbone of AI model can be reported optionally) \|<br>\| #9 Benchmark \| Receiver: non-AI based receiver with realistic assumption Other aspects (including DMRS patterns, etc) can be reported by company The candidate benchmarks can be set as follow: Benchmark 1: Same candidate pattern of overhead-reduced DMRS for sub-case-A (e.g., sparse DMRS) Benchmark 2: Non-overhead-reduced DMRS pattern (e.g., comb-based DMRS pattern) Note: For non-AI receiver, consider realistic channel covariance matrix estimation and realistic noise-and-interference covariance matrix estimation. Note: For sub-case A, companies report at least one of two Benchmarks with the correspondent complexity. Note: For sub-case B, companies report Benchmark 2 and its correspondent complexity. Note: For Benchmark 1, at least comb-based pattern is used considering the maximum number of orthogonal DMRS port (i.e., up to 48 with X=2, where X is TD-OCC length) agreed in RAN1#126. Note: If companies report a comparison between Sub-Case A and Sub-Case B, the report must consider at least benchmark 2 with the correspondent complexity either sub-case A and/or sub-case B. Note: Companies to optionally report an approximate upper bound in performance, e.g. using ideal channel information, when reporting evaluation results. \|<br>\| #9-1 DMRS assumption for AI receiver \| reported by company \|<br>\| #10 Aspects to consider for generalization and scalability performance \| Evaluation Methodologies & Frameworks Evaluation cases 1, 2, 3 in TR 38.843 Case 1: The AI model is trained based on training dataset from one Scenario #A/Configuration #A, and then the AI model performs inference/test on a dataset from the same Scenario #A/Configuration #A. Case 2: The AI model is trained based on training dataset from one Scenario #A/Configuration #A, and then the AI model performs inference/test on a different dataset than Scenario #A/Configuration #A, e.g., Scenario #B/Configuration #B, Scenario #A/Configuration #B. Case 3: The AI model is trained based on training dataset constructed by mixing datasets from multiple scenarios/configurations including Scenario #A/Configuration #A and a different dataset than Scenario #A/Configuration #A, e.g., Scenario #B/Configuration #B, Scenario #A/Configuration #B, and then the AI model performs inference/test on a dataset from a single Scenario/Configuration from the multiple scenarios/configurations, e.g., Scenario #A/Configuration #A, Scenario #B/Configuration #B, Scenario #A/Configuration #B. Companies to report the scenarios/configurations for generalization and scalability verification, e.g., Scenario #A/Configuration #A Scenario #B/Configuration #B Generalization case Dataset mixing method (for Case 3) Note: Companies to highlight the parameter(s) to vary for generalization analysis Channel generation: considering Link Level channel and/or System Level channel (e.g., UMi, UMa) Link Level channel, if used for generating training data, shall be generated with diversified Channel & Environmental Conditions Companies can also use System Level channel to generate training data set Channel & Environmental Conditions Various Channel Types Various Channel parameters, such as various Delay Spread, various Doppler spread, various Angular Distributions Various UE speed Various SNR range Various interference level FFS: how to model interference in LLS Scheduling & System Configurations Different numbers of Tx/Rx antenna ports, MIMO layers, SU/MU-MIMO scenario, and UL precoders (TPMI) Varying time/frequency resource allocation Different MCS & Modulation, if applicable Different DMRS Configurations: DMRS pattern, sequence, time/frequency domain density Power allocation (for Sub-case B) Other aspects are not precluded, and reported by company (if needed) \|<br>\| #11a (Without considering generalization and scalability) parameters for inference \| Reported by company, considering the following aspects aNB antenna configuration UE antenna configuration Channel type Delay spread UE speed SNR range UL Precoder MCS & Modulation TDRA for PUSCH FDRA for PUSCH with PRB size (e.g., 8, 26, or 56) UL waveform (CP-OFDM or DFT-s-OFDM) Other aspects are not precluded, and reported by company (if needed) \|<br>\| #11b-1 SNR gain at BLER target without considering generalization and scalability \| Reported by company Note: Companies should also report the corresponding benchmark. Note: Gain in terms of SNR at the BLER target of PUSCH (i.e., 10%) \|<br>\| #11b-2 Tput gain without considering generalization and scalability \| Reported by company Note: Companies should also report the corresponding benchmark. \|<br>\| #11b-3 NMSE gain (if applicable) without considering generalization and scalability \| Reported by company (optional) Note: Companies should also report the corresponding benchmark. \|<br>\| #11b-4 Net gain (if applicable) without considering generalization and scalability \| Reported by company (optional) Note: Companies should also report the corresponding benchmark. \|<br>\| #12a-1 SNR gain (or loss) at BLER target considering generalization and scalability \| Reported by company Note: The gain or loss of Case 2 and/or Case 3 can be reported considering Case 1 as benchmark. Note: Gain in terms of SNR at the BLER target of PUSCH (i.e., 10%) \|<br>\| #12a-2 Tput gain (or loss) considering generalization and scalability \| Reported by company Note: The gain or loss of Case 2 and/or Case 3 can be reported considering Case 1 as benchmark. \|<br>\| #12a-3 NMSE gain (or loss) (if applicable) considering generalization and scalability \| Reported by company (optional) Note: The gain or loss of Case 2 and/or Case 3 can be reported considering Case 1 as benchmark. \| | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607567](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607567.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| Agreement | For time domain resource allocation for a PUSCH within a slot in 6GR,<br>- Support a single PUSCH mapping type<br>- For starting symbol S and number of symbols L, support S among {0,1,….,13}, L among {1,2,…,14}<br>  - L=1 is supported for CP-OFDM<br>  - FFS: L=1 for DFTS-OFDM depending on the discussion of mapping DMRS RE and PUSCH data RE in a same symbol by FDM manner<br>- Note: DMRS time domain location is discussed separately. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607567](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607567.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| Agreement | For time domain resource allocation for PUSCH across more than one slot in 6GR, at least support single TRP-based PUSCH repetition across multiple slots on a per slot basis,<br>- With the same starting symbol and length per slot.<br>- FFS: Whether/how to additionally support different starting symbol and/or different length per slot. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607567](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607567.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| Agreement | For frequency domain resource allocation for a PUSCH on a single physical carrier in 6GR,<br>- Support RIV-based (“Type-I”) allocation as a physically contiguous allocation method<br>- Study whether/how to support RBG-bitmap-based (“Type-0”) allocation as a physically almost contiguous RB-level (as in 5G NR) allocation method for CP-OFDM<br>  - Companies are encouraged to submit analysis on use cases and simulation results until RAN1#127 for decision on whether it is supported<br>  - Considering impact on UL performance, MPR, scheduling restriction<br>- Study whether/how to support RBG-bitmap-based (“Type-0”) allocation as a physically contiguous allocation method for CP-OFDM and DFTS-OFDM | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607567](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607567.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| Agreement | For RIV-based (“Type-I”) frequency domain resource allocation for a PUSCH on a single physical carrier in 6GR,<br>- Support the following unit as a granularity<br>  - 1 RB<br>  - FFS: Integer multiple of 1 RB<br>  - FFS: whether/how granularity is configurable | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607567](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607567.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |
| Agreement | For the study on downloadable UL codebook for codebook based PUSCH transmission for 6GR, the following is considered for evaluation:<br>- Each precoder matrix , where  is a P-dimensional precoding vector for layer , and  is the number of layers, where P is the number of UE antenna ports.<br>  - (wideband, FFS: subband) is indicated by NW, e.g., via DCI field, from a set of precoder(s) of the downloadable codebook configured to the UE.<br>- Company reports the number of precoders per rank of the downloadable codebook<br>- Companies report how to satisfy UE power constraints (e.g., Using  where its elements are constant modulus)<br>- Companies report which/how coherent types of UL precoder are supported<br>- Companies report the element/vector/matrix quantization assumption, if applicable (e.g., per element phase/amplitude (if any) scalar quantization<br>- Companies report how the NW determines downloadable UL codebook and its correspondent computational complexity when NW determines<br>  - Encourage companies to report the detail procedure of determination of downloadable codebook, and which information is used for determination (if applicable) as much as possible<br>- Companies report signalling overhead, representing format of downloadable codebook, updated frequency of the downloadable codebook, how to determinate when to update | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607567](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607567.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |

| Agreement | Study at least the following two categories of PUCCH formats in 6GR:<br>- Category A: sequence based PUCCH format(s) with or without DMRS<br>  - UCI payload within 1-X bits<br>  - The number of PRBs is 1<br>    - FFS: other values<br>  - FFS: detail on sequence<br>  - FFS: with/without DMRS<br>  - FFS: with/without modulation schemes<br>    - FFS: How UCI bits are carried<br>      - E.g., by modulation<br>      - E.g., by sequence<br>      - E.g., by modulation and sequence<br>  - Note: Channel coding based without DMRS is under this category.<br>  - Note: The X values can be different for PUCCH formats if supported under this category.<br>- Category B: DMRS and channel coding based PUCCH format(s)<br>  - UCI payload larger than Y bits<br>  - The number of PRB for a PUCCH resource is at least configurable by NW<br>    - FFS: Candidate values<br>  - Note: The Y values can be different for PUCCH formats if supported under this category.<br>- FFS: Number of PUCCH formats<br>- The number of OFDM symbols are configurable by NW<br>  - FFS: Candidate values<br>- For each PUCCH format, further study at least the following point(s), if applicable<br>  - Values of X and Y, including whether Y is less than or equal to X<br>  - Waveform<br>  - Modulation schemes<br>  - DMRS design<br>  - Multi-user multiplexing scheme(s)/capability/capacity<br>- FFS: Whether/how to consider MRSS scenario | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607569](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607569.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.5 Multi-antenna system › 10.5.3 CSI acquisition and report

| Agreement | For the 6GR CJT report to facilitate CJT deployment,<br>- Study whether/how to support reporting CJTC delay offset report and codebook-based CSI (with NTRP>1) assuming TRP/panel-specific SD basis selection and TRP/panel-common FD basis selection (if applicable), including:<br>  - Linkage between separate xTRP delay offset report and codebook-based CSI report, whether jointly or separately triggered<br>    - The two reports can be reported in a same reporting occasion or two different reporting occasions<br>  - NW indication of delay offset that UE assumes in codebook-based CSI calculation<br>  - Delay offset is reported together with codebook-based CSI report in one report, whether<br>    - as a part of codebook-based CSI report, e.g., analogous to Rel-18 CJT CSI Mode-1, or<br>      - FFS: precoder frequency granularity due to the delay offset<br>    - not as a part of codebook-based CSI report (in one report, e.g., ri-pmi-cqi-Dd) | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Aspects of DL based CSI acquisition]` |

## 10.5.3.2 Aspects of uplink-based CSI acquisition

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.5 Multi-antenna system › 10.5.3 CSI acquisition and report

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | For multiplexing of SRS ports per resource,<br>- Cyclic-shift (CS) based port is supported without TDM multiplexing for 2-, 3-, 4- and 8-port SRS<br>- Comb-based port multiplexing is supported without TDM multiplexing for 3-, 4- and 8-port SRS<br>- FFS the necessity of TDM multiplexing of cyclic-shift (CS) based port and comb-based port for supporting different number of SRS ports | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For xTyR antenna switching SRS in 6GR,<br>- Prioritize cases where y/x=integer<br>- At least the combinations of x and y as supported in NR shall be supported | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For 6GR, support SRS resource sets, each with one or multiple SRS resources.<br>- An SRS resource can be associated with multiple SRS resource sets.<br>- At least for the following SRS parameters, study whether each parameter shall be common to all SRS resources within the same SRS resource set or resource-specific:<br>  - Time domain behavior (i.e., aperiodic, periodic and semi-persistent)<br>  - Number of ports per SRS resource<br>  - Usage type<br>  - Power control related parameters | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | Study the following combinations of comb value and cyclic shift for comb values less than or equal to 8 (assuming the minimum bandwidth = 4 PRBs for SRS in 6GR)<br>- The maximum number of cyclic shifts up to 24 for comb-2<br>- The maximum number of cyclic shifts up to 12 for comb-4<br>- The maximum number of cyclic shifts up to 6 for comb-8 | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | Study at least the following aspects of TD-OCC:<br>- Candidate covering codes: e.g., Hadamard code, and non-Hadamard codes such as DFT code.<br>- How to design TD-OCC to support all repetition factors.<br>- The performance under partial dropping, and the feasibility of retaining orthogonality-preserved SRS ports under partial dropping, instead of dropping all ports. That is, only the SRS ports that lose orthogonality due to the dropped symbol(s) are discarded, while the remaining SRS ports that can still maintain orthogonality using the surviving symbols are retained for CSI acquisition.<br>- TD-OCC hopping for interference randomization | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For the evaluation of 6GR SRS sequence designs, adopt the following for PAPR/IAPR/CM and XC calculations of baseline NR sequences<br>- CP is not taken into account<br>- value of v and number of sequences<br>- Alt 1: v=0 and 1, all 60 sequences<br>  - All combinations from v=0 and 1 between different sequence groups (u) are measured for XC.<br>  - XC between v=0 and 1 within a sequence group (u) are not measured.<br>- Alt 2: v=0, 30 sequences<br>- IFFT size=8L, where L is the total length considering sequence length and comb structure.<br>- E.g. Msc=96 and comb2 => L=192 (96*2)<br>- Consider Comb=2 only<br>- Adding an NR baseline column to the template for evaluation results collection<br>XC is measured over all delay lags in over sampling domain | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | Study the following combinations of  comb value and cyclic shift for comb values greater than 8 (assuming the following minimum SRS bandwidths for each comb value), including feasibility<br>- For comb-16,<br>  - Option 1: Assuming the minimum bandwidth =8 RBs, CS lengths up to 6 [or 4 or 3]<br>  - Option 2: No cyclic shift applied.<br>  - Option 3: Assuming the minimum bandwidth =40 RBs, CS lengths up to 6 [or 4 or 3]<br>- For comb-12,<br>  - Option 1: Assuming the minimum bandwidth =8 RBs, CS lengths up to 8 [or 6 or 4]<br>  - Option 2: Assuming the minimum bandwidth =6 RBs, CS lengths up to 6 [or 4 or 3]<br>  - Option 3: Assuming the minimum bandwidth =4 RBs, CS lengths up to 4 [or 3]<br>  - Option 4: No cyclic shift applied.<br>  - Option 5: Assuming the minimum bandwidth =32 RBs, CS lengths up to 8 [or 6 or 4]<br>- other values of min SRS BW can be reported | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | Two-level frequency hopping of 6GR SRS, study the following hopping patterns for each level:<br>- First level: hopping over BW with one or multiple frequency hops<br>  - Option 1: SRS subband nested tree-like frequency hopping pattern as in NR<br>  - Option 2: Hopping pattern defined by RB offsets which are derived based on NW indication, details of NW indication is FFS<br>  - Option 3: Sequential hopping patterns<br>- Second level: hopping within each frequency hop of the first-level hopping<br>  - Option 1: SRS subband nested tree-like frequency hopping pattern as in NR<br>  - Option 2: Partial SRS subband sounding with/without intra-repetition hopping (with PF=2 and 4 as baseline)<br>◦            FFS: PF beyond the NR Rel-17 values, e.g., 8 and 16<br>  - Option 3: Subband or partial SRS subband Sequential hopping patterns<br>Note: this does not mean that two-level hopping is supported and single-level hopping is precluded. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For the evaluation of 6GR SRS sequence designs, adopt the following for PAPR/IAPR/CM and XC calculations of sequences,<br>- FFT size=max(8192, 8*sequence length*Comb size). | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For evaluation of 6GR SRS sequence designs, adopt the following assumption on XC calculations for sequences of the same length<br>- XC is measured in time domain over all delay lags<br>- For M number of sequences of the same length, frequency-domain overlapping assumption 1(full overlapping) is used<br>- The magnitude of normalized periodic cross-correlation between distinct sequences  and  at shift   (where ) is computed as<br>where<br>- Paring of M sequences of the same length, M(M-1)/2 pairs in total are taken into account<br>- For each pair of sequences, the cross-correlation metric is defined as the maximum value of the normalized delay cross-correlation spectrum<br>- For M sequences of the same length, collect the mean values of the normalized delay cross-correlation spectrum for all pairs and derive the following statistics: maximum, average, CDF@10%, 50%, 90%. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For evaluation of 6GR SRS sequence designs, adopt the following assumption on XC calculations for sequences with lengths<br>- The position of the desired SRS(victim) sliding in the granularity of 1 RB level within the range of the interfering SRS. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For the study of AI based receiver for SRS overhead reduction, the following template is used as a starting point to collect companies’ results via LLS:<br>\| Parameters \| Value \|<br>\| #1 sub-case \| AI/ML for low-overhead SRS (Sub-Case A): low-overhead SRS with AI/ML at the network side for CSI reconstruction Note: low-overhead SRS may include overhead reduction schemes in the time and/or frequency domains. \|<br>\| #2 Model input \| Reported by company, including input content and input dimension \|<br>\| #3 Model output \| Reported by company, including output content and output dimension \|<br>\| #4 Label \| If applicable, reported by company, including label acquisition method and label error (e.g., zero for ideal label, or non-zero for realistic label) (Labelling error model can be reported optionally) (Cost function can be reported optionally) \|<br>\| #5 Training type \| Offline \|<br>\| #6 Number of SRS ports \| Reported by company \|<br>\| #7 Number of FLOPs (for receiver block replaced by AIML model) \| Reported by company Company also reports inference related parameters for SRS (e.g., number of RBs, symbols, SRS ports) \|<br>\| [#7-1 Number of FLOPs (for receiver block not replaced by AIML model)] \| Number of FLOPs of non-AI receiver blocks corresponding to the candidate receiver assumption(s) in #1 can be reported by company (optional). (if reported, companies to report how to calculate Number of FLOPs of non-AI receiver blocks as well) Note: Non-AI receiver blocks can be: channel estimation, channel interpolation. (FLOPs of additional pre/post processing can be reported optionally, if any) \|<br>\| #8 Number of parameters of AI Model \| Reported by company (Backbone of AI model can be reported optionally) \|<br>\| #9 Benchmark \| Receiver: non-AI based receiver with realistic assumption The candidate benchmarks can be set as follow: Benchmark 1: Same candidate pattern of overhead-reduced SRS for sub-case-A (e.g., SRS with reduced overhead in the time and/or frequency domains) Benchmark 2: Non-overhead-reduced SRS pattern Note: For sub-case A, companies report at least one of two Benchmarks with the correspondent complexity. Note: Companies to optionally report an approximate upper bound in performance, e.g. using ideal channel information, when reporting evaluation results. \|<br>\| #9-1 SRS assumption for AI receiver \| reported by company \|<br>\| #10 Aspects to consider for generalization and scalability performance \| Evaluation Methodologies & Frameworks Evaluation cases 1, 2, 3 in TR 38.843 Case 1: The AI model is trained based on training dataset from one Scenario #A/Configuration #A, and then the AI model performs inference/test on a dataset from the same Scenario #A/Configuration #A. Case 2: The AI model is trained based on training dataset from one Scenario #A/Configuration #A, and then the AI model performs inference/test on a different dataset than Scenario #A/Configuration #A, e.g., Scenario #B/Configuration #B, Scenario #A/Configuration #B. Case 3: The AI model is trained based on training dataset constructed by mixing datasets from multiple scenarios/configurations including Scenario #A/Configuration #A and a different dataset than Scenario #A/Configuration #A, e.g., Scenario #B/Configuration #B, Scenario #A/Configuration #B, and then the AI model performs inference/test on a dataset from a single Scenario/Configuration from the multiple scenarios/configurations, e.g., Scenario #A/Configuration #A, Scenario #B/Configuration #B, Scenario #A/Configuration #B. Companies to report the scenarios/configurations for generalization and scalability verification, e.g., Scenario #A/Configuration #A Scenario #B/Configuration #B Generalization case Dataset mixing method (for Case 3) Note: Companies to highlight the parameter(s) to vary for generalization analysis Channel generation: considering Link Level channel and/or System Level channel (e.g., UMi, UMa) Link Level channel, if used for generating training data, shall be generated with diversified Channel & Environmental Conditions Companies can also use System Level channel to generate training data set Channel & Environmental Conditions Various Channel Types Various Channel parameters, such as various Delay Spread, various Doppler spread, various Angular Distributions Various UE speed Various SNR/SINR range Various interference level FFS: how to model interference in LLS Scheduling & System Configurations Various antenna configurations, e.g., SRS ports, UE Tx port number, UE Tx port layout, number of gNB Rx antennas Various time/frequency domain factors, e.g., various frequency allocations / RPFS / hopping patterns, various time domain allocations, various SRS bandwidth Other aspect, e.g., power of SRS transmission Other aspects are not precluded, and reported by company (if needed) \|<br>\| #11a (Without considering generalization and scalability) parameters for inference \| Reported by company, considering the following aspects aNB antenna configuration UE antenna configuration Channel type Delay spread UE speed SNR range Other aspects are not precluded, and reported by company (if needed) \|<br>\| #11b-1 SNR gain at BLER target \| Reported by company Note: Companies should also report the corresponding benchmark. Note: Gain in terms of SNR at the BLER target of PUSCH or PDSCH (i.e., 10%) based on UL/DL CSI acquisition from SRS \|<br>\| #11b-2 Tput gain \| Reported by company Note: Companies should also report the corresponding benchmark. Note: Gain in terms of Tput of PUSCH or PDSCH based on UL/DL CSI acquisition from SRS \|<br>\| #11b-3 NMSE/SGCS gain \| Reported by company (optional) Note: Companies should also report the corresponding benchmark. Note: NMSE is evaluated on the channel estimation/interpolation output of the SRS receiver. Note: SGCS can be evaluated on the estimated channel and/or on the selected precoder (precoder SGCS). \| | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |

| Agreement | For the mechanisms to achieve time-domain adaptation of PDCCH monitoring for UE energy saving purpose, study at least the following options:<br>- Option 1: use the framework of NR SSSG switching as the starting point to achieve one or more of at least the following<br>  - adaptation of PDCCH monitoring periodicity<br>  - adaptation of monitored search space sets, including dynamic activation/deactivation of search space sets<br>  - adaptation of SSS monitoring duration<br>  - FFS whether the functionality of PDCCH skipping can be achieved under the framework<br>  - FFS joint adaptation between SSSG switching and other parameters<br>- Option 2: dynamic indication of PDCCH skipping<br>- FFS: handling of USS and CSS<br>- Other options are not precluded<br>- Note: the possibility of achieving time-domain adaptation using bandwidth adaptation can be discussed under AI 10.4.2. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2608219](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2608219.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |

| Agreement | Regarding the DCI monitoring size budget at least for single carrier operation,<br>- Xmax = 4 as the starting point.<br>  - Down-select from following counting method,<br>    - Sub-option A: the maximum number of monitored container sizes per slot is limited to Xmax and no additional limitation to the maximum number of monitored container sizes across slots for a UE.<br>    - Sub-option B: the maximum number of monitored container sizes per slot is limited to Xmax , with a separate budget Zmax (> Xmax) to the maximum number of monitored container sizes for a UE across slots, and FFS value of Zmax;<br>    - Sub-option C: the maximum number of monitored container sizes across slots for a UE is limited to Xmax.<br>- No separate per-RNTI budget.<br>Note: The above is at least for single-stage DCI.<br>FFS: For two-stage DCI | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2608219](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2608219.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |
| Agreement | Study necessary DCI fields/payload sizes for cell-specific DCI and DCI for random access procedure, considering necessary functionalities | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2608220](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2608220.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |
| Agreement | On whether/how to support the minimum DCI-to-PDSCH scheduling offset > 0 slot for single-stage DCI, study at least the following options for the triggering of dynamic adaptation of the minimum DCI-to-PDSCH scheduling offset:<br>- Option 1: Explicit dynamic signaling to indicate the minimum DCI-to-PDSCH scheduling offset<br>- Option 2: Implicit rule-based adaptation, configurable by aNB<br>- FFS: joint adaptation with other PDCCH monitoring adaptation techniques<br>- FFS: handling of USS and CSS<br>- Other options are not precluded. | Agreement | For study on the dynamic adaptation of the number of Rx antennas for PDCCH monitoring, consider at least the following options:<br>- Option 1: via explicit indication from the network<br>- Option 2: Implicit rule-based adaptation commonly known to aNB and UE, configurable by aNB<br>- The combination of Option 1 and Option 2 is not precluded.<br>  - Using different options for switching from a larger number of Rx antennas to a smaller number and vice versa is not precluded.<br>- FFS: how NW knows how many Rx antennas UE has, if needed | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2608220](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2608220.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-DL control channel and scheduling]` |

| Agreement | In 6GR, study the following two cases and potential factors to determine the number of DL and UL HARQ processes at least per cell/carrier.<br>- Case 1: the minimum number of HARQ processes at least for TN to be supported by a UE per cell/carrier<br>- Case 2: the maximum number of HARQ processes at least for TN supported per cell/carrier supported by the specification<br>- Potential factors include at least the following:<br>  - Frequency range(s) / SCS<br>  - HARQ RTT for single-carrier and multi-carrier operations (processing/re-scheduling timeline, TDD pattern, duplex mode, number of PUCCH group)<br>  - UE complexity, considering peak data rate and soft-buffer memory (e.g., bandwidth, layers, modulation order)<br>  - Network complexity impact, performance impact and deployment flexibility<br>- FFS: whether / how to limit the number of HARQ processes across cells/carriers<br>- Note: the above does not have implication on the UE capability reporting framework | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2608606](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2608606.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |

| Agreement | For 6GR, study the following methods to simplify the collision-handling procedures for UL channels/signals/UCI:<br>- “One shot” collision-handling based on a time unit (e.g., slot) to avoid iterative process<br>  - FFS the steps for the collision handling for the “one-shot” collision handling procedure.<br>  - FFS channels/signals/UCI involved in the “one-shot” collision handling procedure for a given time unit<br>- Defining a single deterministic reference point for a given time unit for the timeline for processing collision-handling.<br>  - FFS the actual reference point<br>- Defining a deterministic timeline for processing collision-handling<br>  - FFS the actual value(s) of timeline for processing collision-handling<br>- Note: the reference point and timeline in the above are at least applicable to the scheduling/triggering PDCCH<br>  - FFS: applicability to other channels/signals | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Handling of potential multiple uplink transmissions]` |
| Agreement | For the collision handling between PUCCH and PUSCH on different carriers:<br>- For a UE supporting inter-band CA on a band combination, support simultaneous PUSCH and PUCCH transmissions on different carriers in an inter-band CA configuration of the band combination<br>  - Note: RAN4 has agreed “6G FR1 inter-band UL CA power limit will not be constrained by the combined UL band combination power class as in 5G, i.e. PPowerClass,CA specified in clause 6.2A.4.1.3 of TS 38.101-1 will be removed in 6G. Instead, the UE indicates a per-band power class. Each band in the UL configuration (either single carrier or contiguous UL CA), may transmit up to the maximum output power in accordance with the indicated power class for that band.”<br>- Study mechanisms for intra-band CA scenario:<br>  - Intra-band contiguous CA<br>  - Intra-band non-contiguous CA<br>- Study mechanisms for UL Tx switching scenario. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2608575](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2608575.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Handling of potential multiple uplink transmissions]` |

When CLI event is detected, the UE sends notification of occurrence of CLI event in the first PUCCH resource and sends CLI reporting in the associated second PUSCH/PUCCH resource.<br>    - Option 3-3: The UE is provided with a preconfigured PUSCH/PUCCH for UE to report CLI reporting.<br>    - Other option is not precluded.<br>- Note: the reporting overhead, latency reporting reliability and specification complexity of UEI CLI reporting (with pre-configured (if any) or with requested reporting resources)) is to be compared with network-controlled CLI reporting. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
