## 10.6.3 WUS operation with other functionalities

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.6 WUS and operation

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | - For UEs in RRC_CONNECTED state, RAN1 to study at least the following candidate additional functions for DL-WUS,<br>  - Candidate-1: Interaction between DL-WUS and Cell DTX or Cell DRX,<br>  - Candidate-2: DL-WUS interacts with ‘Cell/Carrier Dormancy/(De)activation’ Operation<br>  - Candidate-3: Use DL-WUS as a trigger or indication for on-demand and/or additional RS and/or common signals/channels and/or CSI report, if supported.<br>  - Candidate-4: Study DL-WUS to indicate throughput reduction for subsequent active time<br>  - Candidate-5: DL WUS carries acknowledgement information (ACK/NACK) in response to a UE request for UL data transmission<br>  - Candidate-6: DL WUS indicating beam parameter/TCI update<br>  - Candidate 7: DL-WUS triggers DL-SPS PDSCH reception.<br>  - Candidate 8: DL-WUS activates/de-activates CG-PUSCH transmission.<br>  - Note: 5G NR design is assumed as starting point and further RAN1 and RAN2 progress on relevant features are considered, if applicable.<br>Note: Interaction between DL-WUS and PDCCH monitoring adaption (at least including ‘SSSG switching’ / monitoring-config selection, PDCCH skipping, ‘PDCCH BD reduction’) are to be handled under AI 10.6.1.2. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-WUS operation with other functionalities]` |

## 10.7.1 NTN specific requirements and design

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.7 NTN

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | At least the following parameters for satellite assistance info for UL time-frequency pre-compensation are supported for feeder link in 6GR NTN (at least for GNSS-based operation):<br>- Common TA<br>- Common TA drift rate<br>- Common TA drift rate variation<br>- For the feeder link in a transparent payload case UE assumes one of the following alternatives.<br>  - Alt 1: The feeder link Doppler is fully pre-compensated by the NW as in NR-NTN Rel-17.<br>  - Alt 2: The feeder link Doppler is pre-compensated by the NW in a way that, over the DL on service link, the observed time drift rate matches the observed frequency error.<br>  - Decide which alternative. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |
| Agreement | In 6GR NTN, it is supported that UEs with different GNSS operation modes can operate simultaneously in the same cell.<br>- Network functionality is introduced to control access from UEs in different GNSS operation modes (e.g. to only allow UEs in a given GNSS operation mode to access the cell).<br>  - FFS: Which combination of GNSS operation mode(s) are allowed to be simultaneously enabled in the same cell.<br>  - FFS: Details<br>- NOTE: The operation modes are:<br>  - GNSS-based<br>  - GNSS-degraded<br>  - GNSS-free/GNSS-less | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2608593](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2608593.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |
| Agreement | For the 6GR NTN GNSS-less/free/degraded study, the solutions for UE pre-compensation recommended for specification in TR 38.742 are taken as starting point (except for PRACH physical channel which is discussed in the common agenda):<br>- For open loop uplink time and frequency pre-compensation:<br>  - For scenarios where UE cannot rely on its GNSS for UL timing and frequency pre-compensation on the service link, based on assistance information from the network.<br>  - For cases where last known GNSS location is available and still sufficiently valid, this location is used for uplink open-loop time and frequency pre-compensation<br>    - FFS: How “sufficiently valid” is defined<br>- Frequency adjustment command (in connected mode and RAR)<br>- TA command enhancements at least in RAR<br>- TA drift (e.g. based on FAC)<br>- NOTE: Adjustments to the solutions in TR 38.742 can be proposed based on other decisions taken for 6GR. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2608593](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2608593.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |
| Agreement | For 6GR NTN, further study the following options regarding channel/signal assignment to wide/narrow beams for hierarchical beam structure.<br>- Option 1:<br>  - Use wide beams (in DL) for SSB, common PDCCH, SIBs, paging<br>  - Use narrow beam for PRACH reception at aNB<br>    - Proponents to clarify how the UE selects the PRACH resource for different GNSS operating modes.<br>  - Use narrow beams for dedicated channels (DL + UL)<br>- Option 2:<br>  - Use wide beams (in DL) for SSB<br>  - Use narrow beams (in DL) for common PDCCH, SIBs and paging<br>  - Use narrow beam for PRACH reception at aNB<br>    - Proponents to clarify how the UE selects the PRACH resource for different GNSS operating modes.<br>  - Use narrow beams for dedicated channels (DL + UL)<br>- Option 3:<br>  - Use same beam (wide or narrow) for SSB, common PDCCH, SIBs, paging, PRACH reception at aNB during initial access.<br>  - Hierarchical beam structure is only applied for channels after PRACH reception<br>    - UE’s reporting of beam index can be based on PRACH (e.g. different ROs may correspond to indication of narrow beams), msg3 or subsequent messages. aNB selects the narrow beam for subsequent messages based on this reporting.<br>      - Proponents to clarify details on how the UE selects the beam index for the reporting for different GNSS operating modes.<br>    - Use narrow beams for dedicated channels (DL + UL)<br>- Option 4:<br>  - Use same beam (wide or narrow) for all channels until completion of initial access.<br>  - Hierarchical beam structure is realized without enhancements with respect to NR techniques (for channels after initial access).<br>- Note: For evaluation purposes, RAN1 assumes that the DL of wide beam and the DLs of the narrow beams within the wide beam operate on the same carrier using TDM and/or FDM. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2608595](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2608595.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |
| Agreement | For the 6GR NTN link budget template, for UEs in adverse propagation conditions (APC), “(28e) Other losses” = 9dB.<br>- Other values, i.e. 6 dB and 15 dB maybe optionally used by companies when justified.<br>- The following note from RAN1#126 also applies to this agreement.<br>  - NOTE: The study of a particular scenario for the link budget template does not imply that, if RAN1 determines a coverage gap for that scenario, it will be studied or specified how to close it. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2608595](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2608595.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |
| Conclusion | The xls spreadsheet in R1-2608724 includes a summary of the LLS evaluations for 6GR NTN link budget (for information only). Further discussion in RAN1#127 to collect additional results with potential correction of existing results and discuss how to aggregate results.<br>The following two email discussions are correspondingly assigned.<br>- Post-RAN1#126bis Email discussion 1:<br>  - October 20 – October 29<br>  - Topic: 6GR NTN remaining issues of evaluation assumptions (LLS and SLS)<br>- Post-RAN1#126bis Email discussion 2:<br>  - November 9th – November 13th<br>  - Topic: 6GR NTN link budget LLS results collection | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2608724](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2608724.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |
| Agreement | For 6GR NTN, further discuss the target number or range of HARQ processes | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2608724](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2608724.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-NTN specific requirement and design]` |

## 10.8.1 Evaluations

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.8 ISAC

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | Regarding Rel-20 6G ISAC performance evaluation scope, confirm the working assumption with following revision in red:<br>\| Working Assumption For Rel-20 6G ISAC performance evaluation, evaluate object detection and/or tracking for the following sensing targets/objects: UAV, human, vehicle, AGV discuss further on evaluation on Companies are encouraged to evaluate communication assistance and report at least one of the following: Scope 1: Evaluate performance of sensing measurement/result, which will be used to assist communication Scope 2: Evaluate performance of communication, with and without assistance of sensing measurement/result. \| | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |
| Agreement | For evaluation result collection for object detection and/or tracking, the following information is also collected in the additional tdoc file(s).<br>- Details on how to address non-ideal factor(s) of sensing mode specific assumptions, if non-ideal factor(s) is modelled and then addressed/compensated. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |
| Agreement | Further clarify the radial velocity, in case EO type-2 is involved, as marked in red:<br>\| The true radial velocity is the sum of the following two components: Component 1: The projection of the velocity difference vector on the unit-norm vector from target to STX, where the velocity difference vector is between the true 3D velocity of the target and the true 3D velocity of the STX; Component 2: The projection of the velocity difference vector on the unit-norm vector from target to SRX, where the velocity difference vector is between the true 3D velocity of the target and the true 3D velocity of the SRX. Note: If STx and SRx are static, true radial velocity is , where is the true 3D velocity of the target, is the bistatic angle between incident direction and scattering direction, and is the angle between the true 3D velocity vector and the bisector vector within the bistatic angle . For mono-static sensing, . Note2: Optionally, in case STX-ST link is replaced by STX-EO-ST link (e.g. when STX-ST is in NLOS condition) due to specular reflection from environment object (EO), Component 1 is replaced by the sum of following two components: Component 1a: The projection of the velocity difference vector on the unit-norm vector from EO to STX, where the velocity difference vector is between the true 3D velocity of the EO and the true 3D velocity of the STX; Component 1b: The projection of the velocity difference vector on the unit-norm vector from target to EO, where the velocity difference vector is between the true 3D velocity of the target and the true 3D velocity of the EO; Note3: Optionally, in case ST-SRX link is replaced by ST-EO-SRX link (e.g. when ST-SRX is in NLOS condition) due to specular reflection from environment object (EO), Component 2 is replaced by the sum of following two components: Component 2a: The projection of the velocity difference vector on the unit-norm vector from target to EO, where the velocity difference vector is between the true 3D velocity of the target and the true 3D velocity of the EO; Component 2b: The projection of the velocity difference vector on the unit-norm vector from EO to SRX, where the velocity difference vector is between the true 3D velocity of the EO and the true 3D velocity of the SRX. \| | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |
| Agreement | For Rel-20 6G ISAC performance evaluation, for object detection and/or tracking:<br>- Companies can optionally provide MDP and FAP type-2 calculated as 5G-A ISAC as ‘additional information’ when submitting evaluation results.<br>  - Note: if reported, 5G-A ISAC MDP and FAP type-2 calculation should be based on the same simulation drops that used to calculate 6G ISAC MDP and FAP type-2. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |
| Agreement | For Rel-20 6G ISAC performance evaluation, for object detection and/or tracking:<br>The FAP type 1 (false alarm probability type 1) is calculated as follows:<br>- False alarm probability Type 1 is defined for cases without true target dropped in simulation area. An object detected when there is no target present in the simulation area is considered a false alarm.<br>where,<br>-	 is equal to 1 if at least one object is detected when there is no target dropped in the simulation area in the drop n, otherwise  is equal to 0.<br>-	 is the total number of drops without target in the simulation area.<br>The horizontal/vertical positioning accuracy is defined as follows:<br>- Horizontal/vertical positioning accuracy is defined as the absolute value of the difference between the estimated horizontal/vertical position and the corresponding true position of a sensing target.<br>Note: The value of horizontal/vertical positioning accuracy and the velocity accuracy shall be obtained assuming 90% confidence level, which is the 90th percentile point of the CDF of the location estimation error. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |
| Conclusion | Regarding reporting RMSE of power of path(s) for SAC-Scope 1:<br>- Do not report RMSE of power of path(s) for SAC-Scope 1 in AI 10.8.1.<br>Note: No intension to hinder the power related study in other ISAC agendas, e.g. study of RSRP/SINR/amplitude/phase of a path, power related information for scattering point/target, etc. for either OD&T or SAC in AI 10.8.2.<br>Note2: This does not preclude companies to simulate and report SAC-Scope 2 evaluation by utilizing power of selected path(s). | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |
| Conclusion | Regarding reporting RMSE of phase of path(s) for SAC-Scope 1:<br>- Do not report RMSE of phase of path(s) for SAC-Scope 1 in AI 10.8.1.<br>Note: No intension to hinder the phase related study in other ISAC agendas, e.g. study of RSRP/SINR/amplitude/phase of a path, etc. for either OD&T or SAC in AI 10.8.2.<br>Note2: This does not preclude companies to simulate and report SAC-Scope 2 evaluation by utilizing phase of selected path(s). | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |
| Agreement | For Rel-20 6G ISAC performance evaluation, for sensing assisted communication, for reporting the time-frequency resource cost due to sensing for SAC:<br>- Type_1: Resource that are used for sensing purpose (due to sensing for SAC)<br>- Type_2: Part of Type_1 resources that are used for communication purpose<br>- Type_3: Resources that are not used for sensing purpose, and cannot be used for communication purpose due to sensing operation (due to sensing for SAC)<br>Companies are required to report Overhead 1 and Overhead 2.<br>- Overhead 1: (Type_1 + Type_3) resources over all radio DL and UL resources<br>- Overhead 2: (Type_1 - Type_2 + Type_3) resources over all radio DL and UL resources<br>Note: If Type_2 resource doesn’t exist, two overheads are the same; if Type_1 resource = Type_2 resource, Overhead 2 is equal to Type_3 resource only, which could be zero if Type_3 doesn’t exist.<br>Note 2: Overhead 1 and Overhead 2 should be calculated in NW level, not single UE level.<br>Note 3: For some of the communication use cases, the communication resource overhead may be reduced due to sensing assistance, which will be reported separately, e.g. as performance metric of communication<br>Note 4: Type_2 resource shall be calculated excluding the reduced communication resource overhead if the communication resource overhead can be reduced due to sensing assistance.<br>Companies can report additional information/assumption on whether/how much the SAC resource can be shared with OD&T.<br>Note 5: This does not require companies to simulate OD&T and SAC simultaneously<br>Note 6: This does not imply specifying same or different RS/resource for OD&T and SAC | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607430](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607430.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |
| Agreement | The additional performance metrics are considered in SAC-Scope 1, in addition to those agreed in RAN1#126.<br>For enrivonment (large surface, wall) that reconstructed by sensing<br>- Candidate 2-1: Dihedral angle, i.e. the angle between the reconstructed plane and the ground-truth plane.<br>Note: When evaluated, companies are not required to report every metric. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607430](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607430.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |
| Agreement | Templates (excel files) in R1-2608660 are adopted to collect evaluation results of sensing assisted communication in AI 10.8.1.<br>Additional tdoc file(s) will also be used to collect companies input in details, which may include but not limited to:<br>- Details on sensing RS/resource<br>- Details on inter-site/intra-site interference modelling, if modelled<br>- For Scope 2, details on how the sensing measurement/result is able to assist communication<br>- For environment reconstruction of Scope 1, details on how to reconstruct the plane from the detected specular reflection points, e.g., point discard/selection, plane-fitting, usage of prior information, etc.<br>Note: Further revision is not precluded, depending on future discussion | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607431](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607431.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |
| Agreement | Endorse the draft LS R1-2608734 with following update.<br>---<br>Based on the above parameters, the PSD can be inferred. However, due to the sensing-RS resource mapping being company-reported parameter, the RAN1 study does not define a common sensing-signal PSD.<br>Regarding activity factor (sensing resource ratio) and distribution over time, in RAN1 these are up to companies’ discretion, striving for meeting the sensing performance requirements. In 5G-A, the range of sensing resource ratio   is from around 1% to 28% for TRP monostatic sensing. In 6G ISAC evaluations, the sensing overhead which cannot be shared with communication is encouraged to be below 10%. Note that in both 5G-A and 6G, no common time-domain sensing resource pattern was agreed in RAN1.<br>---<br>Final LS in R1-2608735 is endorsed. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607431](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607431.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |

## 10.8.2 Aspects of integration with communication

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.8 ISAC

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | - The following interference types need to be studied for 6G ISAC, which are dependent on sensing modes and sensing scenarios.<br>  - Self-interference (for the case where mono-static is involved)<br>  - The interference between sensing signals<br>  - The interference between sensing and communication<br>- Companies are encouraged to bring up evaluation results on potential interference issues.<br>  - E.g., what is interference source, in which sensing mode, performance difference with/without interference are recommended for the study.<br>- If interference issue for sensing needs to be resolved, the following aspects of interference control can be studied in Rel-20, taking interference handling for communication as starting point.<br>  - Coordinated resource allocation in time/frequency/code/spatial domain, including e.g., measurement/transmission gap/window<br>  - Resource muting<br>  - Rate matching<br>  - Power control<br>  - Interference resource/measurement/report<br>  - Implementation method<br>  - Other aspects are not precluded | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | - Study at least the following beam related aspects for Rel-20 ISAC taking communication beam management as starting point, to identify whether sensing specific enhancement on beam related aspects for sensing is necessary or not<br>  - Measurement/report/information to determine best Tx-Rx beam pair(s) for sensing<br>  - QCL/TCI state or spatial-relation assumptions of sensing RS | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | - Study at least the following aspects of open loop power control of UE as sensing transmitter using existing SRS power control framework as starting point, to identify whether sensing specific enhancement on power control for sensing is necessary or not<br>  - P0/alpha<br>  - PCMAX<br>  - Power boosting<br>  - Pathloss/Pathloss reference<br>  - PH/PHR<br>  - Association of the applicable power-control parameters for sensing purpose<br>  - UE transmission power within CPI<br>  - the impact/interference to communication and sensing<br>- Companies are encouraged to bring up evaluation results on potential power control issues. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607231](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607231.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Working Assumption | - RAN1 adopts the following definition on nominal Coherent Processing Interval (CPI) for discussion purpose<br>  - “nominal CPI”: The time interval for sensing measurements during which the sensing RS resource can be coherently processed unless intolerable phase discontinuity and/or power inconsistency happens.<br>    - FFS: in nominal CPI, the sensing RS resource can be coherently processed in time domain only or in other domain as well<br>    - FFS: whether/how both transmitter and receiver have information on nominal CPI<br>    - FFS: solutions to maintain/recover the phase continuity and/or power consistency in a nominal CPI<br>    - FFS: details on intolerable phase discontinuity and/or power inconsistency<br>  - Note: the use nominal CPI in RAN1 discussion doesn’t necessarily mean such terms will be specified in normative phase | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607231](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607231.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | The following options can be considered for further study on the delay measurement of a path of path-based measurement or of a sample of profile-based measurement.<br>- Option 1: Receive time difference between a path/sample and a reference path/sample,<br>  - is the receive time defined by the path/sample<br>  - is the receive time defined by the reference path/sample<br>    - FFS Candidates of reference path/sample<br>- Option 2: Receive time difference between a time of path/sample and a reference time,<br>  - is the receive time defined by the path/sample<br>  - is reference time. FFS reference time<br>- Option 3: Receive time of a path/sample of a node minus a transmission time of the same node,<br>  - is the receive time defined by the path/sample<br>  - is a transmit timing. FFS transmit timing<br>- [Option 4: Receive time of a path/sample minus a transmission time,<br>  - is the receive time defined by the path/sample<br>  - is a transmit timing. FFS transmit timing] | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607232](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607232.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | - Study the following options on AOD (angle of departure) related measurement of a path/sample,<br>  - Option 1: Absolute measurement of the path/sample<br>  - Option 2: relative measurement<br>    - FFS details<br>- Study the following options on measurement<br>  - Option A1: angle of AOD of a path/sample<br>  - Option A2: amplitude and phase per Tx antenna port of a path/sample<br>  - Option A3: indirect information of AOD of a path/sample<br>  - Option B1: RSRP/SINR/beam ID/resource ID of one or multiple Tx beams of a path/sample<br>    - FFS beam ID/resource ID<br>  - Option B2: amplitude, phase, ratio of amplitude and phase, and beam ID/resource ID of one or multiple Tx beams of a path/sample<br>    - FFS beam ID/resource ID<br>  - Adding other option is not precluded<br>  - Note: applicability to STX/SRX/SF and sensing modes should be separately discussed | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607232](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607232.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | - At least the following events that may impact phase continuity and/or power consistency in a nominal CPI are encouraged to be studied from RAN1 perspective<br>  - TDD UL/DL switching<br>  - beam switching<br>  - frequency allocation change, including frequency hopping, 6GR-BW / CC change<br>  - change of Tx power<br>  - change of TA via TAC and/or ATA<br>  - Tx/Rx chain reconfiguration<br>  - [Target/STX/SRX mobility]<br>  - [RRC state change]<br>- Study the necessity and impact to handle phase discontinuity and/or power inconsistency issue in a nominal CPI from RAN1 perspective. Companies are encouraged to bring up evaluation results in RAN1#127<br>  - Option 1: RS to recover phase continuity and/or power consistency<br>  - Option 2: use of a reference path to recover phase continuity and/or power consistency<br>  - Option 3: RS with overlapping resources in frequency domain<br>  - Option 4: implementation-based solutions<br>  - Option 5: Postpone/drop update of impacted parameters by event causing phase discontinuity and/or power inconsistency<br>  - Option 6: exchange of information on events<br>  - Option 7: Separated to multiple parts of nominal CPI for sensing measurements<br>  - Option 8: Drop certain sensing measurements in the nominal CPI<br>  - Option 9: Phase continuity and/or power consistency in the nominal CPI are always assumed<br>  - Option 10: Prioritize sensing over other procedure<br>  - Other options are not precluded<br>- Study the tolerable Phase discontinuity and/or power inconsistency in a nominal CPI from RAN1 perspective. Companies are encouraged to bring up evaluation results in RAN1#127 | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607233](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607233.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | Study at least the following aspects for a sensing RS resource consisting of one or multiple component RS<br>- The multiple component RS have the same [QCL, Tx beamforming, spatial transmit filter, spatial transmit reference, antenna port(s)] and can be assumed to be transmitted coherently | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607233](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607233.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | Study at least the following aspect of a sensing RS resource consisting of one or multiple component RS<br>- RE pattern of the union set of the one or multiple component RS can be uniform or non-uniform RE pattern<br>  - FFS details | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607233](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607233.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |

| Working Assumption | - For waveform and frame structure evaluation, the following common parameters are used as baseline for comparison of the evaluation results across companies.<br>- Other parameters not described below can be used from the agreed SLS simulation assumption in ISAC evaluation agenda and reported by company.<br>- Note: As long as the parameters listed in the table are necessary for evaluation, the values of the parameters are recommended to be commonly used.<br>- Note: Not all parameters listed in the table can be used for evaluation, depending on the evaluation methodologies (LLS, a simplified SLS, and waveform characteristics analysis).<br>- Note: when the delay associated with target changes within a CPI, the metrics related to ambiguity function can be calculated according to a smaller CPI value with constant delay.<br>\| Evaluation case \| Low-High Mobility, Long range \| Low-Moderate Mobility, Moderate range \| Low Mobility, Short range \|<br>\| Carrier frequency (fc) \| 7 GHz \| 4 GHz \| 30 GHz \|<br>\| Bandwidth \| 100 MHz \| 100 MHz \| 400 MHz \|<br>\| Waveform \| CP-OFDM \| CP-OFDM \| CP-OFDM \|<br>\| SCS \| 30 kHz \| 30 kHz \| 120 kHz \|<br>\| CP length mode \| Normal CP \| Normal CP \| Normal CP \|<br>\| Number of sensing symbols within CPI \| 256 \| 64 \| 64 \|<br>\| Sensing RS comb size (RE) \| 1 \| 1 \| 1 \|<br>\| Freq. comb size for effective CP length extension (only for frame structure evaluation) \| 4 \| 4 \| 4 \|<br>\| Number of successive symbols for effective CP length extension (only for frame structure evaluation) Note: Combination of comb size and symbol repetition is not mandated. \| 2 \| 2 \| 2 \|<br>\| Sensing symbol periodicity \| 3 symbols \| 13 symbols \| 40 symbols \|<br>\| ISD \| 1732 m \| 500 m \| 20 m \|<br>\| Min. mono-/bi-static range \| 20 m \| 4 m \| 2 m \|<br>\| Max. mono-/bi-static range \| 3504 m \| 500 m \| 20 m \|<br>\| Min. Sensing Target radial velocity \| 3 km/h \| 3 km/h \| 1 km/h \|<br>\| Max. Sensing Target radial velocity \| 320 km/h \| 140 km/h \| 3 km/h \|<br>\| Sensing target RCS Note: Combination of RCS and other parameters should be aligned with the agreed SLS assumption in AI 10.8.1. \| RCS based on Clause 7.9.2.1 in TR 38.901 \| RCS based on Clause 7.9.2.1 in TR 38.901 \| RCS based on Clause 7.9.2.1 in TR 38.901 \|<br>\| Benchmark RS \| Information \| Information \| Information \|<br>\| RS for UE transmission \| NR UL SRS (ZC sequence) \| NR UL SRS (ZC sequence) \| NR UL SRS (ZC sequence) \|<br>\| RS for TRP transmission \| NR DL CSI-RS (Gold sequence) \| NR DL CSI-RS (Gold sequence) \| NR DL CSI-RS (Gold sequence) \|<br>Note: as example, the source of parameters: first column from UAV, second column from vehicle, third column from human case. | [Chair notes RAN1#126bis_eom1](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23126bis_eom1.docx)<br>[R1-2607997](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_126b/Docs/R1-2607997.zip)<br>`[126bis-R20-6GR-ISAC-Waveform and FS]` |
